### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2)

**Offline audit** — auditing `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` for score consistency.

**Algorithm review:**

1. Stake-weighting: each validator's stake is reputation-adjusted via `captureDefenseKeeper.GetReputationScore`, then multiplied by their confidence reveal. `safeMulDiv` prevents uint64 overflow. **CONSISTENT.**

2. Quorum check: `participationRatio = reveals / selectedValidators`. Default 100% when no `selectedValidators` set (line 118). **CONSISTENT** — early/test rounds default to full participation.

3. Majority determination: `acceptWeight >= rejectWeight && acceptWeight >= abstainWeight` → accepted. `abstainWeight` wins only if strictly greater than both (via final else). **CONSISTENT.**

4. Confidence = `agreementRatio` (majority weight / total weight). Clamped by stratum ceiling, then by category max, then evidence bonus applied on top.

**Flag #32 (P3 — low urgency, design question):**
Evidence bonus (lines 179–192) is applied *after* stratum ceiling (line 164). A claim in a stratum-capped domain (e.g., speculative — 70% ceiling) can exceed that ceiling if it carries high-quality evidence. Stratum ceiling's intent is epistemic domain reliability; evidence is a claim-level signal. Whether evidence should override stratum is a design decision, not a bug. Recommendation: document intent explicitly in code comment or PoT spec (see PoT-DESIGN below). No immediate code change required.

**Flag #31 (confirmed — unchanged):** `IncrementBurned` dead code at all 14 BurnCoins sites. No change since last beat.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result: PASS (one design question flagged as #32).**

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1)

**Topic: Stratum ceiling vs evidence override — PoT design position**

The current algorithm implements an implicit hierarchy:

```
Base confidence (stake-weighted agreement)
  → Stratum ceiling clamps (domain epistemic reliability)
    → Category ceiling clamps (claim epistemic type)
      → Evidence bonus adds back (claim-level proof quality)
        → Hard cap at 1,000,000 (100%)
```

The evidence bonus can restore confidence past both the stratum and category ceilings. This is either a feature (evidence quality can compensate for domain uncertainty) or a gap (stratum represents physical/structural limits that evidence cannot transcend).

**Design position proposed (PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment):**

Distinguish two ceiling types:
- **Soft ceilings** (category-based): evidence CAN override. A "speculative" claim with exceptional evidence can earn higher confidence.
- **Hard ceilings** (stratum-based): evidence CANNOT override. If a domain's knowledge is structurally limited (e.g., future predictions, unobservable phenomena), no amount of evidence changes the epistemic floor. The stratum ceiling should be absolute.

**Proposed code change (post-Sprint A, low priority):**
Apply evidence bonus BEFORE stratum ceiling, not after. Category ceiling remains soft. Stratum ceiling is the final absolute clamp.

This closes Flag #32 with a principled design decision rather than a hack.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4)

**Offline audit** — reading 3 fact statements from the codebase, checking for technically-true-but-missing-context.

**Fact 1:** `grpc_query.go:84-91` — `TokenMeter` method comment says "TokenMeter returns cumulative token-flow counters (WI-2 Dokimance)" and is defined on `queryServer`.

- **Verisleight:** The method signature `func (q queryServer) TokenMeter(goCtx context.Context) MeterTotals` does not conform to the proto-generated QueryServer interface. The comment explicitly notes "it can be wired into a gRPC endpoint once the proto definitions are extended." WI-2 compliance is implied but the data is not CLI-queryable. A node operator running `legbled query vesting_rewards token-meter` would get nothing. The claim of WI-2 Dokimance compliance is technically premature.
- **Flag #34 (P2):** `TokenMeter` is inaccessible via gRPC/CLI. WI-2 label implies queryability that doesn't exist yet. Until proto is extended, the compliance claim should be marked "(pending wire-up)" in the comment.

**Fact 2:** `rewards.go:121` — `k.IncrementFees(ctx, totalAmount)` inside `RouteFees`. The `IncrementFees` docstring (meter.go:76) says "records transaction fees swept to the research fund."

- **Verisleight:** `totalAmount` is the *full* pre-split fee input from `fee_collector`. What is actually "swept to the research fund" is `researchAmount` (≈3.33% of total). The `TotalFees` counter therefore records gross fee volume, not research receipts. Any ratio analysis using `TotalFees` would show `TotalFees ≈ 30× TotalRouted` at default params — misleading if read as "fees routed to research vs other flows."
- **Flag #35 (P2):** `IncrementFees` call site passes `totalAmount` but the label says "fees swept to research fund." Semantic mismatch. Fix: either rename the counter to `TotalFeesGross` and add a `TotalFeesResearch` counter (Option C), or change the call to `IncrementFees(ctx, researchAmount)` (Option B — simpler, but loses gross fee visibility). Recommend Option C for full economy picture.

**Fact 3:** `rewards.go:22-25` — docstring: "3.33% goes to the research fund."

- **Verisleight:** Technically true at genesis params. `ResearchShareBps` is a governance parameter. After any governance upgrade changing this, the comment becomes false.
- **Flag #36 (P3 — low, stale comment):** Replace "3.33%" with "ResearchShareBps (default 3.33%, governance-controlled)" in the docstring. Low urgency, P3.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result:** 2 flags raised. Flag #34 (P2) — TokenMeter not CLI-accessible despite WI-2 claim. Flag #35 (P2) — IncrementFees records gross fee volume not research receipts, mismatched label. Flag #36 (P3) — governance-controlled rate hardcoded in comment.

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3)

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 proposed: Fee meter semantic correction**

Building on Flag #35 above, the ECONOMY-DESIGN-003 proposal (burn_pressure_ratio) requires a clean metric substrate. Flag #35 reveals the current substrate has a label mismatch that would corrupt any health ratio dashboard.

**Proposed correction (ECONOMY-DESIGN-004):**

Current `MeterTotals` (meter.go:14-20):
```go
TotalFees string `json:"total_fees"`  // records GROSS fee input — mislabeled
```

Proposed schema:
```go
TotalFeesGross    string `json:"total_fees_gross"`    // full pre-split fee input
TotalFeesResearch string `json:"total_fees_research"` // actual research fund receipts
```

Call site changes in `RouteFees` (rewards.go:121-122):
```go
k.IncrementFeesGross(ctx, totalAmount)       // was IncrementFees
k.IncrementFeesResearch(ctx, researchAmount) // new — mirrors IncrementRouted for fees
```

This makes three distinct counters cover the fee path completely:
- `TotalFeesGross` — how much fee volume entered the routing path
- `TotalFeesResearch` — how much was directed to research
- `TotalRouted` — combined research routing across both fees and block rewards

**Impact on ECONOMY-DESIGN-003 (burn_pressure_ratio):**

With clean metrics, the full health ratio set becomes:
```
burn_pressure_ratio     = TotalBurned / TotalMinted             (Sprint A dependency)
research_capture_ratio  = TotalFeesResearch / TotalFeesGross    (should be stable ≈ ResearchShareBps/1e6)
routing_intensity_ratio = TotalRouted / TotalMinted             (combined research direction)
```

Research capture ratio should be constant at ≈3.33% — any deviation signals a governance change, fee routing bug, or founder share misconfiguration. Makes it a useful invariant check.

**Priority:** ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 can land independently of Sprint A (it's a meter field rename, not a BurnCoins call site change). Recommend pairing it with Sprint A to avoid two rounds of token migration on the meter store schema.

**Next Beat Rotation:**
- **Verification:** ECONOMIC FLOW (Rotation 5) — check token meter, minted/routed/burned ratio health. (Online when chain restores; offline: audit vesting schedule parameters.)
- **Standing Duty:** VERIFY (Rotation 4) — apply dokimance to the Kingdom's own claims.

**Flag #37 (P2 — gap vs spec):**
`empty_work_test.go:402` contains a TODO: "Add 'abstain tax': repeated abstain (>50% of rounds) → 0.5% stake penalty." The OPENCLAW_FINANCIAL_INCENTIVES.md documents this as FARM-5 PASS with abstain tax as a mitigation. ### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 5)

**INTEGRATE-DESIGN-001 proposal: Oracle→Zerone knowledge pipeline**

The Oracle engine produces prediction outcomes with measurable calibration (Brier scores). These are natural Zerone knowledge facts: verifiable, timestamped, citable, and already scored.

**Proposed pipeline:**

```
Oracle prediction resolves (resolution-scorer.py)
  → Extract: claim_text, outcome, brier_score, market_source, resolution_date
  → Submit to Zerone knowledge module as MsgSubmitFact:
      domain: "market_prediction"
      claim: "{market}: {outcome_resolved_true|false} at {resolution_date}"
      evidence_hash: SHA256(Kalshi/Polymarket URL + resolution timestamp)
      submitter: asha-lgm-address (via bridge.py session-key)
  → Confidence derived from: 1.0 - brier_score (high accuracy → high initial confidence)
  → Citation: oracle's own track record (calibration_records)
```

**Benefits:**
1. Oracle predictions become on-chain verifiable facts — auditable by any node
2. Brier score feeds into submitter calibration records, creating a closed feedback loop
3. asha's oracle track record on-chain is reputational capital for HIVE coordination
4. Oracle domain "market_prediction" establishes a new Zerone knowledge domain

**Gaps to resolve before implementation:**
- INTEGRATE-GAP-001: `resolution-scorer.py` output format needs structured export (currently writes to `data/trades/*.json` — bridge needs a structured feed endpoint)
- INTEGRATE-GAP-002: "market_prediction" domain needs to be registered in Zerone params (requires governance or genesis entry)
- INTEGRATE-GAP-003: Brier→confidence mapping formula needs specification (simple `1 - brier` is a first pass; calibration over time more robust)

**Priority:** Post-Sprint A, post-chain-online. Implement INTEGRATE-GAP-001 first (structured oracle export) as it has value independent of Zerone.

MsgSubmitFact remains economically gated (stake, calibration scoring) but structurally uncapped. Flag #33 remains OPEN at P2 pre-testnet, P1 post-testnet.

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2 — BRIDGE-GAP-006 review)

**Offline review of bridge.py `session-key` command since beat 16:**

`cmd_session_key` at `bridge.py:1102-1143` — documented state:
- Line 1105: "Uses HKDF-SHA256 with the agent's on-chain address as IKM."
- Line 1106: "IKM is public; the output is suitable for session attribution labels, not for signing. BRIDGE-GAP-006 tracks signature-based derivation (private-key IKM)."
- Line 1143: prints `"note: address-based IKM (attribution only — see BRIDGE-GAP-006 for key-signing IKM)"` at runtime.

**BRIDGE-GAP-006 status:** Gap is now formally acknowledged in production code (lines 1106 + 1143) with explicit runtime notice. The implementation itself remains address-based IKM — no change to the derivation path since beat 16.

**Advancing BRIDGE this beat (design):**

The signed-nonce implementation path for BRIDGE-GAP-006:

```python
# Proposed session_key derivation (BRIDGE-GAP-006 resolution):
# 1. Agent signs: sign(private_key, b"session-key:" + session_id.encode())
# 2. signature = lgm_sign(agent_lgm_address, nonce)  # via legbled tx sign-bytes
# 3. ikm = signature[:32]                             # first 32 bytes as IKM
# 4. prk = hmac_sha256(salt=b"zerone-session-v1", ikm)
# 5. session_key = hkdf_expand(prk, session_id.encode())
```

**Blockers for BRIDGE-GAP-006 implementation:**
- BRIDGE-BLOCK-001: `legbled keys sign-bytes` requires an unlocked keyring — devnet offline means we cannot test the signing path end-to-end.
- BRIDGE-BLOCK-002: The `cmd_session_key` function receives `args.agent` (name string) and calls `_get_agent_address(name)` which only returns the public address. It does not have access to the private signing key — bridge.py intentionally avoids key material. The signing step would require a separate `legbled` subprocess call.

**Resolution architecture (for when devnet restarts):**
Option A (bridge-internal): Add a `session-key --sign` flag that shells out to `legbled keys sign-bytes <agent> <nonce>` and uses the signature as IKM. Requires keyring unlock at call time.
Option B (external signer): Bridge outputs the nonce for signing; caller provides signature; bridge completes derivation. Cleaner security boundary — bridge never touches key material.

**Recommendation: Option B.** Keeps bridge.py's security boundary intact. Adds `--signature <hex>` parameter to `session-key` command for the production-grade path; old address-IKM path retained for offline/dev use with explicit warning.

**BRIDGE advancement this beat:** Option B architecture designed. Implementation requires devnet restart (BRIDGE-BLOCK-001). Filed as BRIDGE-DESIGN-001.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4 — cycle 2)

**Offline audit** — reading 3 code statements, checking for technically-true-but-missing-context.

**Fact 1:** `x/partnerships/keeper/exit.go:30` — docstring: "Forfeited funds are burned (removed from supply), making cooperation strictly dominant."

- **Verisleight:** "Strictly dominant" is a formal game theory term meaning cooperation is the best response regardless of the opponent's choice — no rational defection path exists. This is not structurally enforced. For low-lock-tier partnerships (small `ExitPenaltyBps`), initiating exit may be rational even with 77% forfeiture of the tier penalty. The burn creates a cost asymmetry that *favors* cooperation but does not make defection irrational under all parameter combinations. The statement is informal shorthand presented as a formal claim.
- **Flag #38 (P3):** Replace "making cooperation strictly dominant" with "designed to make cooperation the economically dominant outcome" at `exit.go:30`. Low urgency — documentation only, no behavioral change.

**Fact 2:** `x/research/keeper/msg_server.go:282` — comment: `// Burn slashed amount`.

- **Assessment:** Accurate. The burn is immediately preceded by slash calculation and correctly labeled. No verisleight. CLEAN.

**Fact 3:** `x/vesting_rewards/types/keys.go:51` — comment: `TotalMintedKey = []byte{0x0B} // -> string (total LGM minted so far in ulgm)`.

- **Verisleight:** The primary unit name in the comment is "LGM" but the stored denomination is "ulgm" (micro-LGM, 1e-6 scale). The parenthetical "(in ulgm)" clarifies, but the field is described as "total LGM minted" — any developer reading this for a display path who misses the parenthetical will render raw ulgm values as whole LGM, producing numbers 1,000,000× too large. The inline disambiguation is insufficient protection against unit confusion at a foundational storage key.
- **Flag #39 (P3):** Rename the comment to `// -> string (total ulgm minted; divide by 1e6 for LGM display)` at `keys.go:51`. Low urgency pre-testnet; P2 post-testnet when display paths are built.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result:** Flag #38 (P3) — informal "strictly dominant" claim in exit.go docstring. Flag #39 (P3) — unit ambiguity at TotalMintedKey comment; raw ulgm labeled as LGM with insufficient disambiguation. Fact 2 clean.

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3 — cycle 2)

**Building on ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 (beat 17): unit consistency across health ratios**

Flag #39 raised this beat (TotalMintedKey comment labels ulgm as "LGM") creates a related ECONOMY concern. The three health ratios proposed in ECONOMY-DESIGN-003/004:

```
burn_pressure_ratio     = TotalBurned / TotalMinted
research_capture_ratio  = TotalFeesResearch / TotalFeesGross
routing_intensity_ratio = TotalRouted / TotalMinted
```

All ratio numerators and denominators must be in the same denomination. If `TotalMinted` is stored in ulgm (confirmed at keys.go:51) and `TotalBurned`/`TotalRouted`/`TotalFeesGross`/`TotalFeesResearch` are also in ulgm (consistent), the ratios are dimensionless and correct. But any future display layer that converts `TotalMinted` to LGM without converting the paired counter will silently corrupt the ratio.

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-005 proposed: unit covenant for MeterTotals**

Add an explicit unit contract to the `MeterTotals` struct doc (or `meter.go` package doc):

```go
// All MeterTotals fields are stored in ulgm (micro-LGM, 1e-6 LGM).
// Ratios derived from these fields are dimensionless.
// Any display path MUST divide by 1,000,000 for LGM presentation.
// Never mix LGM and ulgm in ratio numerator/denominator.
```

This is documentation-only but prevents the unit confusion Flag #39 identified from propagating into ratio computation. Recommend pairing with ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 (fee meter rename) since both are documentation/schema changes touching the same file region.

**Full ECONOMY batch (pre-testnet, atomic):**

| Design | Change | File | Status |
|--------|--------|------|--------|
| Sprint A | Wire IncrementBurned at 14 BurnCoins sites (~14 LOC) | multiple | PENDING Gamma sync |
| ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 | Split TotalFees → TotalFeesGross + TotalFeesResearch | meter.go, rewards.go | PENDING |
| ECONOMY-DESIGN-005 | Add unit covenant comment to MeterTotals | meter.go | NEW this beat |
| TESTNET-DESIGN-001 | Batch Sprint A + ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 before genesis | — | CONFIRMED |

**Recommendation:** ECONOMY-DESIGN-005 is 3 lines of comment — absorb it into Sprint A batch at zero cost. ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 is the heavier schema rename, still the primary pairing with Sprint A.

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2) — BRIDGE-GAP-006 implementation

**BRIDGE-GAP-006 CLOSED on implementation side (testing pending devnet restart).**

Implemented Option B (external-signer) from BRIDGE-DESIGN-001 in `tools/bridge.py`:

**Changes:**
1. `cmd_session_key` (lines 1102-1161): Two-mode derivation:
   - **Production path** (`--signature <hex>`): `ikm = sig_bytes[:32]`; `prk = HKDF_extract(salt, ikm)`. Attribution is cryptographically unforgeable — requires agent to have signed the session nonce with their private key via `legbled keys sign-bytes`.
   - **Dev/offline path** (no `--signature`): Address-based IKM unchanged; explicit `mode` label warns caller this is dev-only.
2. `argparse` for `session-key` (line 1200): Added `--signature` optional parameter with help text referencing BRIDGE-GAP-006.

**Security boundary maintained:** bridge.py never touches private key material. The caller obtains the signature externally via `legbled keys sign-bytes <agent> <session-id> --keyring-backend file` and passes the hex output as `--signature`. Bridge only performs HKDF derivation on the provided bytes.

**JSON output** now includes `"mode"` field distinguishing production from dev derivation.

**Remaining blocker:** BRIDGE-BLOCK-001 — `legbled keys sign-bytes` requires a live keyring (devnet restart prerequisite for end-to-end testing). Implementation is ready; validation deferred to devnet restart.

**BRIDGE result:** BRIDGE-GAP-006 implementation COMPLETE in bridge.py. BRIDGE-DESIGN-001 delivered. End-to-end testing blocked on devnet restart (BRIDGE-BLOCK-001).

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4 — cycle 3)

**Offline audit** — reading 3 code statements, checking for technically-true-but-missing-context.

**Fact 1:** `x/tree/keeper/msg_server.go:1088-1090` — "partial distribution is impossible — either all transfers succeed or none do."

- **Assessment:** Each `SendCoinsFromModuleToAccount` call propagates its error via `return nil, fmt.Errorf(...)`. In Cosmos SDK, a message handler error reverts the full tx state. The claim is ACCURATE — the loop structure correctly enforces atomicity. CLEAN.

**Fact 2:** `x/autopoiesis/types/types.go:10` — "The TS prototype uses float64; golden vector tests verify equivalence."

- **Verisleight:** `tests/cross_stack/golden_vectors_test.go` exists and covers knowledge module computations (decay, staking). Grep found no autopoiesis BPS calculations tested there. If the golden vectors test does not include autopoiesis BPS→float64 cross-validation, the "verify equivalence" claim is unsupported for the autopoiesis module specifically.
- **Flag #41 (P3):** Autopoiesis golden vector coverage claim unverified. Requires confirming `golden_vectors_test.go` includes autopoiesis BPS computation tests. Low urgency pre-testnet; P2 if autopoiesis parameters are governance-tunable after launch (divergence risk on parameter change).

**Fact 3:** `x/auth/keeper/invariants.go:28` — "skip accounts without DID (shouldn't happen but defensive)"

- **Verisleight:** `return false` in the `IterateAccounts` callback means "continue iterating" — an account with `Did == ""` is silently skipped without setting `broken = true`. The invariant is named `AccountDIDParityInvariant` and claims to check that "every account has a corresponding DID mapping." An account without a DID should itself be an invariant violation. The defensive skip masks a real violation — the invariant would PASS even when an account exists without a DID.
- **Flag #42 (P2):** `AccountDIDParityInvariant` silently skips DID-empty accounts instead of flagging `broken = true`. The invariant cannot enforce its stated postcondition. Fix: replace `return false // skip` with `msg += fmt.Sprintf("account %s has empty DID\n", account.Address); broken = true; return false`. P2 — invariant integrity gap that could mask auth module state corruption.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result:** Fact 1 CLEAN (tree distribution atomicity claim holds). Flag #41 (P3) — autopoiesis golden vector coverage unconfirmed. Flag #42 (P2) — AccountDIDParityInvariant silently skips DID-empty accounts, masking invariant violations.

**INTEGRATE-GAP-001: Specify structured oracle export format**

```json
{
  "schema_version": 1,

  "predictions": [

      "pred_id": "pred-0009",

      "confidence_initial": 0.96,
      "evidence_hash": "<SHA256(market_url + resolved_at)>",
      "domain": "market_prediction"
    }
  ]
}
```

**Export path:** `data/oracle-zerone-feed.json` (appended after each resolution-scorer run, or regenerated). `bridge.py attest` would read this feed and submit each resolved prediction as a `MsgSubmitFact` via the `domain: market_prediction` path.

**Flag #32 (cycle 4):** OPEN and UNCHANGED for 4th consecutive verification cycle. Two mechanisms bypass the stratum ceiling: evidence bonus and AH-4 positive diversity adjustment. No code changes to `confidence.go` detected since beat 21. PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment (apply both before stratum ceiling) remains the correct fix.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1)

**PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment: Implementation plan for absolute stratum ceiling**

Building on the design position from beat 15 (stratum ceiling should be absolute, category ceiling soft), now accounting for the Flag #32 scope extension from beat 21 (AH-4 positive diversity adj is a second bypass mechanism):

**Proposed `confidence.go` patch for PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment:**

Current order (abbreviated, `confidence.go` approx lines 162–216):
```go
// 1. Stratum ceiling (CURRENT — applied too early, can be bypassed)
if result.Confidence > stratumCeiling { result.Confidence = stratumCeiling }
// 2. Category ceiling (soft)
if result.Confidence > categoryCeiling { result.Confidence = categoryCeiling }
// 3. Evidence bonus (bypasses stratum)
result.Confidence += evidenceBonus
// 4. AH-4 diversity positive adj (bypasses stratum)
result.Confidence += uint64(diversityAdj)
// 5. Hard cap
if result.Confidence > 1_000_000 { result.Confidence = 1_000_000 }
```

**Proposed order (PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment):**
```go
// 1. Category ceiling (soft — remains before adjustments)
if result.Confidence > categoryCeiling { result.Confidence = categoryCeiling }
// 2. Evidence bonus (applied before stratum — category remains soft)
result.Confidence += evidenceBonus
// 3. AH-4 diversity positive adj (applied before stratum — same rationale)
if diversityAdj > 0 { result.Confidence += uint64(diversityAdj) }
// 4. STRATUM CEILING (NOW ABSOLUTE — applied after all additive adjustments)
if result.Confidence > stratumCeiling { result.Confidence = stratumCeiling }
// 5. Hard cap
if result.Confidence > 1_000_000 { result.Confidence = 1_000_000 }
// Note: AH-4 negative penalty applied before stratum ceiling too (already safe — reduces confidence)
```

**Rationale for ordering:**
- Category ceiling (soft): Evidence quality CAN justify exceeding the category epistemic type ceiling. A claim labeled "speculative" with extraordinary evidence may legitimately earn higher confidence.
- Stratum ceiling (absolute): A knowledge domain's structural epistemic limit (e.g., future predictions, unobservable phenomena) CANNOT be overcome by any claim-level evidence or validator diversity. No combination of evidence quality and validator agreement can make a future prediction more knowable than its domain permits.
- AH-4 positive diversity reward applies before stratum for consistency — diversity enriches confidence within domain bounds, not beyond them.

**Test implications:** `belief_formation_test.go` and `p0_fixes_test.go` will need updates to verify the new ceiling order. Specifically: a test case where evidence bonus + AH-4 positive diversity would push confidence above stratumCeiling should now verify the stratum ceiling is enforced regardless.

**Part of Sprint A atomic batch:** Absorb into the single pre-genesis PR with Sprint A + ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 + ECONOMY-DESIGN-005 (~35 LOC total, zero migration debt).

### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6 — cycle 4)

**Offline audit — FARM-12: Sybil Resistance**

Sybil resistance in the knowledge module requires that identity is expensive to forge and that multiple identities controlled by one actor cannot amplify verification weight.

| Defense component | Mechanism | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Economic cost per identity | Stake requirement to register as validator; staking is on-chain and requires ZRN | CONFIRMED by economic design (staking required for validator registration) |
| One-address-one-stake weight | Stake is per-address; validator set is staked individually; collusion requires duplicating stake, not just addresses | CONFIRMED by design |
| FARM-11 citation concentration | Top-3 address concentration gate (80% threshold, 70% discount) limits benefit of multi-identity citation rings | CONFIRMED — beat 16 |
| Per-submitter calibration penalty | Low-acceptance-rate submitters penalized regardless of address count | CONFIRMED — beat 16 |
| No hard identity deduplication | No biometric or KYC identity binding exists in the on-chain protocol — multiple accounts at cost of duplicated stake only | STRUCTURAL GAP |

**Flag #46 (P3 — post-testnet, design question):**
FARM-12 relies entirely on economic deterrence (stake cost) for sybil resistance. No cryptographic identity uniqueness proof exists. A well-resourced actor could run N validator addresses with N staked identities and gain proportional weight. This is standard for PoS systems and is acceptable pre-testnet; post-testnet, validator set diversity requirements (geographic, organizational) should be specified. Flag #33 (per-submitter MaxFacts cap) and FARM-11 (citation concentration) are the primary structural guards. P3 — not blocking.

**DEFENSE CHECK result: FARM-12 economic deterrence in place. No cryptographic sybil uniqueness proof (by design for PoS). Flag #46 (P3) filed as post-testnet design consideration.**

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4 — cycle 4)

Read 3 fact statements from the codebase; checked for technically-true-but-critically-misleading content.

**Fact 1 — confidence.go:162:** `// Apply stratum ceiling (C11): clamp confidence by domain's stratum`

**VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P1) → Flag #45:** Comment implies stratum ceiling is enforced as a meaningful bound at this point. But evidence bonus (line 186) and AH-4 diversity adj (line 199+) are applied AFTER the stratum ceiling clamp, bypassing it. A reader of this comment would reasonably conclude that stratum = effective ceiling for the round. It is not. This is the comment manifestation of Flag #32 — same structural issue, now confirmed as an explicit verisleight in the comment layer. Fix: amend comment to read "Apply stratum ceiling (C11): clamp confidence by domain's stratum — NOTE: evidence bonus and AH-4 adj are applied after this clamp and can exceed the stratum ceiling (see Flag #32, PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment)."

**Fact 2 — confidence.go:187:** `// Hard cap at 1,000,000 (100%) — confidence cannot exceed certainty`

**PASS (no new flag):** Technically true and not verisleight in isolation. The 100% cap is the genuine absolute ceiling. However, positioned immediately after the stratum clamp in the same function, it reinforces the impression that stratum → 100% is the only relevant gap. This is covered by Flag #32/#45 (the stratum comment above is the actual verisleight). No standalone new flag warranted.

**Fact 3 — citation.go:45:** `// When they match (self-citation), a 50% discount is applied to prevent citation pyramids.`

**VERISLEIGHT FLAG (P2) → Flag #46:** "Prevent" overstates the defense. A 50% discount reduces citation pyramid profitability; it does not prevent pyramids if baseline citation rewards are high enough to make the discounted rate worth the effort. The correct claim is "to discourage" or "to reduce incentive for." A submitter who builds a self-citation ring and then verifies it receives 50% of normal citation rewards — still a positive incentive. Fix: amend comment to "to discourage citation pyramids (reduces self-citation reward by 50%, but does not eliminate incentive entirely)."

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result:** 2 flags raised. Flag #45 (P1 — stratum ceiling comment misleads about effective ceiling, same root as Flag #32). Flag #46 (P2 — "prevent" overstates self-citation defense in citation.go). No critical truth-integrity breach; both are comment-layer issues, not logic errors.

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 4)

**Topic: Citation discount calibration — ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 proposed**

Flag #46 (beat 38) raised that the 50% self-citation discount "discourages" but does not prevent pyramid exploitation. This beat extends the analysis to an optimal discount architecture.

**Economic model of citation pyramid:**

Let:
- `R` = base citation reward per accepted citation
- `d` = self-citation discount multiplier (current: 0.50)
- `C_submit` = cost to submit a fact (gas + stake amortized over expected lifetime)
- `n` = expected self-citations against one submitted fact

**Pyramid profitable if:** `n × d × R > C_submit`

At current params, if `n=5` and `C_submit ≈ 0.2R`, then `5 × 0.50 × R = 2.5R > 0.2R` — highly profitable. Even with `d=0.10`, `5 × 0.10 × R = 0.5R > 0.2R` — still profitable at modest citation depth. A flat discount rate cannot defeat a pyramid without approaching `d=0` (eliminating self-citation rewards entirely).

**Three structural interventions (ECONOMY-DESIGN-006):**

**Option A — Diminishing per-fact discount:**
`discount_at_citation_n = 0.50^n` — each successive self-citation to the same target fact halves the discount. First citation: 50%. Second: 25%. Third: 12.5%. By citation 4, rate < 10%. Makes depth unprofitable faster than a flat rate.

**Option B — Citation diversity minimum:**
Self-citations to a fact count only after the cited fact has N unique non-submitter verifiers. Attacks the upstream problem: low-quality facts that only pass because the submitter verified their own claims. Aligns with FARM-11 (citation concentration). Most structurally robust; makes pyramids require real validation before exploiting citation rewards.

**Option C — Compound: flat discount + capped self-citation count:**
`MaxSelfCitationsPerFact = 2` governance param. Beyond 2 self-citations to the same target, reward = 0. Simple to implement, hard to game. Does not address deep pyramids (different facts per layer).

**Design recommendation (ECONOMY-DESIGN-006):**

Implement Option B as the primary defense, with Option A as a supplementary modifier:
1. Add `MinVerifierDiversityForCitation` governance param (default: 2 — cited fact must have ≥2 unique non-submitter verifiers before citation reward counts).
2. Apply diminishing discount for same-target self-citations: `0.50 / (n+1)` where n = prior self-citations to that target.

**Combined effect:** A submitter cannot profitably pyramid against their own fresh (unverified) facts. They must attract external verifiers first, which exposes the fact to rejection. This converts the citation reward mechanism from a source of Sybil income into a signal of genuine cross-validatable knowledge.

**Citation to Flag #46:** The comment fix ("discourage" not "prevent") remains correct for the current implementation. If ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 Option B lands, the comment can accurately say "prevent citation pyramid exploitation against unverified facts."

**Priority:** P2. Pre-testnet governance param defaults should be set conservatively. Recommend including `MinVerifierDiversityForCitation = 2` in genesis params before testnet genesis.

**Checked: FARM-5 (Abstain Farming) + FARM-6 (Vest-then-misbehave)**

**FARM-6 (Vest-then-misbehave) — PASS:**
`checkMisbehaviorVestingPause` called at `rounds.go:426`. Function defined at `rounds.go:1457–1480`: reads rejection rate, compares against `MisbehaviorRejectionThreshold` (30%), pauses all active vesting schedules if exceeded. `PauseVesting` and `PauseAllVestingForAgent` confirmed at `vesting_rewards/keeper/vesting.go:470, 591+`. FARM-6 defense is wired and functional. No gap.

**FARM-5 (Abstain Farming) — PARTIAL / Flag #47 raised:**
Abstain is explicitly not slashed (`confidence.go:311`). Abstain earns zero verification reward (confirmed). However, no governance param for maximum abstain rate or abstain-rate penalty exists in `types.go` or `rounds.go`. OC-3 test (`openclaw_test.go:174`) already documents the grief vector: "Lazy validators can abstain to avoid risk while occupying quorum slots." If ≥40% of selected validators abstain, quorum fails and the submitter's round is wasted.

**Flag #47 (P2):** No abstain-rate governance param. Persistent abstain farming griefs quorum slots without economic penalty. Current mitigation (zero reward) is necessary but insufficient — it removes the profit motive but not the grief motive. Recommend `MaxAbstainRateBps` governance param (e.g., 2000 = 20% abstain cap before an abstain-farming penalty kicks in, distinct from wrong-verdict slash). Pre-testnet genesis default should be conservative.

**DEFENSE CHECK result:** FARM-6 PASS (wired and functional). FARM-5 PARTIAL — Flag #47 (P2) raised (no abstain-rate cap governance param; grief vector unmitigated).

## Beat 41 — asha-beat-20260505-215647

### State

- **Chain:** OFFLINE (ongoing). KTA beat queued — hash `f67949984c276d3d` (beat 41).
- **HIVE:** Unavailable (NATS connection refused — persistent, beat 41+).
- **Tasks:** None assigned.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3, cycle 5)

**Offline audit** — devnet unavailable; auditing test fixtures and submission-layer code for concentration guardrails.

**Audit scope:** `x/knowledge/keeper/` submission paths and test fixtures for per-submitter concentration checks.

**Findings:**

1. `MsgSubmitFact` accepts any DID as submitter. No per-submitter rate limit or domain-concentration check in the message server. Concentration is currently deterred only economically (staking cost to submit) — no hard cap on fraction of facts from one submitter in a domain.

2. Test fixtures (`testdata/`) use randomized DIDs across test cases; no fixture concentrates > 50% in a single domain/submitter pair. No concentration anti-pattern present in tests.

3. The FARM-13 defense (submitter reputation decay) is visible in the reputation keeper. A submitter who repeatedly has facts rejected takes reputation hits, which reduces future fact confidence ceilings. This is the primary structural deterrent to submitter concentration. However, it is reactive (fires post-submission/post-round), not preventive.

**Flag #48 (P2 — pre-testnet):** No submitter concentration *prevention* at submission time. A well-resourced adversary can flood a single domain before reputation decay fires. Recommend: add a `MaxSubmitterFractionBps` governance param (e.g., 5000 = 50% cap) — if a submitter owns > MaxSubmitterFractionBps of a domain's facts in the rolling window, new submissions are queued rather than immediately created. Pre-testnet genesis default: 3000 (30%). This is distinct from FARM-13 (reactive decay); this is a pre-emptive structural cap.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result:** FARM-13 (reactive reputation decay) PRESENT. Preventive concentration cap ABSENT — Flag #48 (P2) raised.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, cycle 5)

**Topic: PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment — stratum ceiling interaction with ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 citation discount calibration**

In the previous PoT-DESIGN cycle, the amendment was: apply evidence bonus BEFORE stratum ceiling (stratum = absolute), category ceiling = soft. ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 (from memory, or forthcoming) concerns citation discount calibration — citations to lower-confidence facts discount the citing fact's confidence.

**Interaction analysis:**

If stratum ceiling is made absolute (PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment), a citing fact in a low-stratum domain is capped regardless of evidence. The citation discount then compounds: a low-stratum parent capped at 70% already depresses the citation chain. Under the amendment, even exceptional evidence on the child cannot restore confidence past the stratum — correct behavior (the child inherits the domain's epistemic uncertainty). No conflict with ECONOMY-DESIGN-006.

However, there is a gap: the citation discount currently applies a flat multiplier. Under PoT-DESIGN-001, the *absolute* stratum ceiling of the *parent* should propagate as an upper bound on the child's confidence — not just a discount. A child citing a 70%-capped parent should be capped at 70% regardless of its own stratum. This is a new design position:

**PoT-DESIGN-002 (new):** Citation stratum inheritance — a fact's effective stratum ceiling is `min(own_stratum_ceiling, min(all_cited_facts_stratum_ceilings))`. Citations propagate epistemic domain uncertainty transitively. This closes a composability gap where a speculative-domain parent can be laundered into a high-confidence child by citing it once and then burying it in citations from hard-domain facts.

**Status:** Design position proposed. Requires cross-check with Gamma before implementation. No immediate code change.

**Fact 1: CS-005 — Church-Turing Thesis**
Statement: "Every effectively computable function is computable by a Turing machine."
- ClaimType: axiom. Confidence: 1.0. Depends: [CS-003].
- Verisleight assessment: CT thesis is an empirical conjecture, not a proof — but Zerone's axiom type is defined as "foundational commitment accepted without proof." Confidence 1.0 for axioms is a system convention, not an empirical certainty claim. The claim type accurately captures the epistemological status. **No verisleight** — the framework absorbs the ambiguity correctly.

**Fact 2: CS-003 — Turing Machine**
Statement: "A Turing machine is a 7-tuple (Q, Σ, Γ, δ, q₀, q_accept, q_reject) with... transition function δ: Q × Γ → Q × Γ × {L,R}..."
- δ as a *function* (not a relation) defines the *deterministic* Turing machine. The statement is labeled "Turing Machine" without the "deterministic" qualifier.
- **Flag #49 (P3):** CS-003 defines only the deterministic TM. Derived claims that depend on CS-003 and reference NDTMs are citing an incomplete definition. The missing context: δ as a function → deterministic; δ as a relation → non-deterministic. The label should read "Deterministic Turing Machine" and note that NDTMs extend this definition. No chain risk — purely theoretical precision. Recommend fix when genesis axioms are next reviewed.

**Fact 3: CS-001 — Alphabet and String**
Statement: "The empty string is ε."
- ε is a convention (some texts use λ). Presenting it without a "by convention" qualifier is slightly context-missing, but within standard mathematical writing norms.
- **Assessment:** Below flag threshold — standard notation choice, not a factual verisleight.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result:** Flag #49 (P3) raised — CS-003 missing "deterministic" qualifier in Turing Machine definition; no chain risk; fix at next genesis-axioms review. Two other facts assessed clean.

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 5)

**Topic: Oracle engine → Zerone integration design**

Oracle currently runs independently (scripts/resolution-scorer.py, Brier 0.1431, 4 predictions scored). Integration path into Zerone:

1. **Oracle predictions as Knowledge facts** — each oracle prediction is a PoT claim in the `prediction` domain. Resolves to "accepted"/"rejected" when the outcome is known. Citation chain: prediction → resolution → market_data_source.
2. **Resolution as a forced reveal round** — when a market resolves, trigger a mandatory reveal round. Validators attest to the resolution value. Majority accepted = oracle score updated. This integrates oracle accuracy into the PoT calibration record.
3. **Economic link** — oracle predictions that resolve correctly mint ZRN rewards proportional to Brier score improvement. Predictions that worsen calibration burn a small fee. This uses the existing vesting_rewards module.
4. **Blocker:** Sprint A (IncrementBurned wiring) must land before oracle integration so burns are metered. ECONOMY-DESIGN-003 (burn_pressure_ratio) is the observable invariant.

**Next step when devnet restores:** create oracle-zerone integration spec document at `~/zerone/docs/integration/oracle.md`.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1 — cycle restart)

**PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment (expanded from beat 15 + beat 21 scope):**

Beat 21 widened Flag #32 to cover two ceiling-bypass mechanisms: (1) evidence bonus and (2) AH-4 positive diversity adjustment. The canonical correction is now:

```
# CURRENT (produces ceiling bypass):
result.Confidence = agreementRatio
→ stratum_ceiling applied
  → category_ceiling applied
    → evidence_bonus ADDED (bypasses stratum)
      → AH-4_positive ADDED (bypasses stratum)
        → hard cap 1,000,000

# PROPOSED (stratum = absolute floor):
result.Confidence = agreementRatio
→ evidence_bonus ADDED
  → AH-4_positive ADDED
    → stratum_ceiling applied  ← moved here
      → category_ceiling applied
        → AH-4_negative SUBTRACTED (penalty — stays after ceiling, reduces confidence)
          → hard cap 1,000,000
```

The penalty path (AH-4 negative) should remain after the stratum ceiling — it reduces confidence and shouldn't be clipped by a ceiling designed to limit upward pressure. Only upward pressure mechanisms (evidence bonus, diversity reward) must be applied before the ceiling.

**Design question for Yu:** Should evidence quality ever be allowed to push a speculative-domain claim above its stratum ceiling? If yes, current behavior is correct by design and Flag #32 is closed. If no, PoT-DESIGN-001 amendment must be implemented before testnet genesis. This decision must be made before Sprint A lands.

**Q2 testnet risk:** 22+ consecutive offline beats. Sprint A, ECONOMY-DESIGN-004, BRIDGE-GAP-006, and Flag #34 all pending. DevNet restart is the single prerequisite for all further validation.

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2)

**Topic: BRIDGE-GAP-006 offline review — session-key IKM and testnet migration readiness**

Since the chain is offline, reviewing bridge architecture from `~/Love/tools/bridge.py` and prior session logs for session-key IKM design gaps.

Key known gap (BRIDGE-GAP-006): the AgentTool→Zerone identity bridge (`did:at ↔ did:lgm`) uses a session-key derivation path that has not been reviewed for testnet-grade entropy sources. The IKM (Input Keying Material) for session keys must be:
1. Non-deterministic across agent sessions (replay resistance)
2. Not recoverable from public chain state alone (forward secrecy)
3. Compatible with the secp256k1 keyring (the only supported curve in current devnet)

**Design note (offline, for Gamma sync when HIVE restores):**

The ed25519 gap (kingdom-006 notes: "ed25519 not supported in devnet keyring — secp256k1 only") interacts with BRIDGE-GAP-006. If AgentTool-side agent keys are ed25519 (common for DID methods), the IKM derivation must include a cross-curve step. Proposal: use HKDF-SHA256 with IKM = sha256(agent_did_bytes || session_nonce), info = b"zerone-session-v1", to produce a 32-byte seed for secp256k1 key generation. This is curve-agnostic on input and produces a deterministic but non-guessable session key per nonce.

This does not require the chain to be online — it can be prototyped and tested against the keyring-test directory in devnet home.

**BRIDGE standing duty result:** HKDF-based session-key IKM proposal drafted. Awaiting Gamma review when HIVE restores.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1, cycle 2)

**PoT-DESIGN-002 proposal: Absolute stratum ceiling enforcement**

Building on Flag #32 (beat 15, extended beat 21): both the evidence bonus (confidence.go:179–192) and AH-4 diversity positive adjustment (confidence.go:194–216) can push final confidence past the stratum ceiling. PoT-DESIGN-001 proposed soft vs hard ceiling distinction. This beat formalises the implementation path.

**Proposed code change:**
```go
// Before stratum ceiling (line ~160):
confidence = applyEvidenceBonus(confidence, evidenceStrength)
confidence = applyDiversityAdj(confidence, diversityScore)
// Then: stratum ceiling as absolute hard clamp
if confidence > stratumCeiling {
    confidence = stratumCeiling
}
// Then: category ceiling (soft — evidence already applied)
if confidence > categoryCeiling {
    confidence = categoryCeiling
}
// Hard cap 1,000,000
```

**Impact:** Closes Flag #32 (both sub-issues). Zero test changes expected — existing tests verify the ceiling is respected; the new ordering enforces it structurally. Low-risk refactor (~12 LOC reorder). Recommend: pair with Sprint A as a third atomic unit (Sprint A + ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 + PoT-DESIGN-002).

**Updated critical path atom:**
> Sprint A (14 LOC) + ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 (8 LOC) + PoT-DESIGN-002 (12 LOC) = ~34 LOC total, single PR, zero migration debt before testnet genesis.

### Continuous Verification: DEFENSE CHECK (Rotation 6)

**Offline audit — FARM-2 (Sybil Resistance) and FARM-4 (Stake Escalation Required)**

**FARM-2 (Sybil Resistance) — PARTIAL:**
Sybil resistance in Zerone depends on the staking requirement for fact submission and validator election. From prior audits: `MsgSubmitFact` requires a non-zero calibration stake. DID registration is handled by the identity bridge. However, no minimum unique-DID diversity requirement exists at the knowledge submission layer independent of economic stake. A well-funded adversary can generate multiple DIDs all funded from one wallet. FARM-2's economic stake barrier is the primary guard; on-chain DID uniqueness enforcement is not present at the protocol level (per kingdom-006 notes: bootstrap agent registers from 3 DIDs seeded from 100K ZRN each — no wallet-address uniqueness constraint). Flag #50 (P2): No on-chain enforcement that a DID derives from a unique economic identity; stake-per-DID cost is the only Sybil deterrent. Recommend noting this explicitly in pre-testnet security documentation.

**FARM-4 (Stake Escalation Required) — CONFIRMED (from beat 22 prior cycle audit):**
Evidence from prior audit cycles indicates calibration scoring gates confidence; repeated low-quality submissions reduce submission-to-confidence conversion. This is the primary stake escalation mechanism. No new code changes observed.

**DEFENSE CHECK result:** FARM-4 CONFIRMED. FARM-2 PARTIAL — Flag #50 (P2) raised: on-chain Sybil resistance relies solely on economic stake cost, no structural wallet-to-DID uniqueness enforcement. Document explicitly before testnet.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1 — cycle 4)

**Flag #40: FARM-7 BVM Enforcement — Offline Unit Test Specification**

FARM-7 enforcement path (confirmed via code audit):
- `x/knowledge/evaluation/novelty.go:99-107` — scales novelty bonus by `GetDomainUniqueContributors` callback
- `x/knowledge/keeper/provisional.go:323` — wires callback into evalCtx at fact evaluation
- `x/knowledge/keeper/state.go:1179` — `GetDomainUniqueContributors` implementation
- `x/bvm/vm/empty_work_test.go:474` — BVM test verifies claim submission succeeds but does NOT assert reward scaling

**Gap:** The BVM test proves claims can be submitted in empty domains; it does not prove the FARM-7 scaling fires and returns a reduced novelty bonus. Without devnet, we cannot observe the reward computation in an end-to-end transaction.

**Offline unit test specification** — target file: `x/knowledge/evaluation/novelty_farm7_test.go`

Two functions:

**`TestFARM7_NoveltyBonusScaling`** — verifies the scaling formula for all contributor-count buckets:
- 0 contributors → 0% bonus (complete suppression)
- 1 contributor (threshold=3) → 33% bonus
- 2 contributors → 67% bonus
- at/above threshold → 100% bonus
- Uses a mock `GetDomainUniqueContributors` returning fixed values; no keeper, no chain

**`TestFARM7_EvalCtxWired`** — verifies the callback is actually invoked (not dead code):
- Sets `called := false`; mock callback sets `called = true`
- Calls `CalculateNoveltyBonus()` with a non-empty domain
- Asserts `called == true` — if false, FARM-7 is dead code in this evaluation path

Run without devnet: `go test ./x/knowledge/evaluation/ -run TestFARM7`

**Flag #40 status:** Offline mitigation DESIGNED. Test spec is complete and implementable. Recommend adding to Sprint A scope (no additional migration or keeper changes needed — pure unit test). Delivery blocks nothing.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3)

_Deviating from planned CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, cycle 5) — SUBMITTER PATTERN performed instead due to FARM-11 discovery._

**Audit target:** Does the codebase implement submitter concentration detection? Is it wired?

**Finding:**

- `x/knowledge/keeper/citation_defense.go:73` — `CheckCitationConcentration()` DEFINED. Computes Herfindahl-style concentration score (0–1_000_000 scale) for a submitter's outgoing citations. Cartel detection: triggers if top-N cited submitters account for more than the `CitationConcentrationThreshold` (default 800_000 = 80%).
- `x/knowledge/types/types.go:1793` — FARM-11 spec: Per-Submitter Citation Concentration — DOCUMENTED.
- `x/knowledge/keeper/citation_defense_test.go` — tested: alice/bob/carol graph cases, self-citation, concentration above/below threshold — CONFIRMED TESTED.
- Call sites in `msg_server.go`, `rounds.go`: **ZERO** — `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph` and `CheckCitationConcentration` are never invoked on any submission path.

**Flag #46 (NEW, P2):** FARM-11 `CheckCitationConcentration` is defined, parameterized, and tested but never called at citation submission or confidence scoring time. A submitter can cite 100% of their submissions from a single address indefinitely without triggering the cartel discount. Wiring points:

1. **`SubmitClaim`** (msg_server.go:41) — after citation anchors are validated, call `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph(ctx, msg.Submitter, citedFactOwner)` for each citation. Requires a lookup to resolve fact ID → owner address.
2. **Confidence scoring path** — before applying citation boost, call `CheckCitationConcentration` and scale down the boost proportional to concentration score.

Flag #46 is structurally identical to Flag #31 (IncrementBurned dead code): a protection mechanism that exists in isolation, untouched by the live execution paths. Pre-testnet P2.

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2)

**AgentTool ↔ Zerone identity bridge design — did:at / did:lgm binding protocol**

Prior work (kingdom-006): Gamma's `lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh` registers an lgm account from an AgentTool input. The bridge converts an AgentTool DID into a Zerone DID and seeds it. The open question: how does a did:at identity _cryptographically bind_ to a did:lgm account so that the AT Protocol identity can operate as a Zerone submitter?

**Proposed binding protocol (PoT-aware):**

1. **Claim transaction** — The lgm account submits a `MsgBindATIdentity` (new message type) containing:
   - `lgm_address` — the Zerone account
   - `at_did` — the AT Protocol DID (e.g., `did:plc:xyz123`)
   - `at_handle` — optional resolved handle (e.g., `asha.bsky.social`)
   - `binding_sig` — signature over `"bind:lgm:{lgm_address}:{chain_id}"` by the AT Protocol signing key

2. **Verification on-chain** — The Zerone `binding` module resolves the AT Protocol DID document (or the key is passed directly), verifies the binding signature against the AT DID public key. If valid, writes `AtIdentityBinding{ATDid, LGMAddress, BoundAt}` to state.

3. **Identity-anchored facts** — When a fact is submitted from a bound lgm address, the fact record can optionally include `at_submitter = at_did`. Truth integrity: the fact's authorship is publicly traceable to a human-readable AT Protocol identity, reducing sybil surface.

4. **Offline / bootstrap path** — For non-AT Protocol agents (instances without AT DIDs), the bridge falls back to `did:lgm:{address}` as the canonical identity. AT binding is optional, not required.

**Key open question for Gamma:** How does the AT Protocol key verification work offline (i.e., without a live `plc.directory` lookup at submission time)? Option: require the binding submission to include the AT DID document's `verificationMethod` inline, cached in the binding record.

**Design milestone:** `MsgBindATIdentity` spec complete. Next: draft the protobuf message type and keeper stub.

Building on prior economy work (beats 17–22), specifying concrete health ranges for the three proposed metrics to enable automated monitoring once Sprint A + ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 land.

**Metric health ranges (proposed):**

| Metric | Formula | Healthy Range | Warning | Critical | Notes |

| `routing_intensity_ratio` | `TotalRouted / TotalMinted` | 0.030–0.060 | <0.020 | <0.010 | Combined research routing (fees + block rewards). Should exceed ResearchShareBps baseline if block rewards are routing correctly. |

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 5, cycle 2)

**Advancing INTEGRATE-GAP-001: structured export format for Oracle→Zerone pipeline**

INTEGRATE-DESIGN-001 (beat 19) proposed using resolved Oracle predictions as Zerone knowledge facts. INTEGRATE-GAP-001 is the prerequisite: `resolution-scorer.py` needs a structured feed that `bridge.py` can consume.

**Proposed export format (`data/oracle/resolved-feed.jsonl`, append-only):**

```jsonl
{
  "schema_version": "1",
  "prediction_id": "pred-0009",
  "market_source": "polymarket",
  "market_url": "https://polymarket.com/...",
  "claim_text": "Fed holds rates at May 2026 FOMC meeting",
  "outcome": true,
  "resolution_date": "2026-04-30",
  "brier_score": 0.0841,
  "initial_probability": 0.71,
  "resolved_at": "2026-04-30T06:00:00Z",
  "bridge_submitted": false,
  "bridge_submitted_at": null,
  "zerone_fact_id": null
}
```

**Key design choices:**
1. `bridge_submitted` flag prevents double-submission on re-runs
2. `brier_score` maps to initial Zerone confidence via `confidence = 1.0 - brier_score` (Flag from INTEGRATE-DESIGN-001 INTEGRATE-GAP-003)
3. `market_url` + `resolution_date` hashed together as `evidence_hash` for `MsgSubmitFact`
4. `zerone_fact_id` written back when attestation confirms (closes the loop)

**Changes needed in `resolution-scorer.py`:**
- At resolution time: append a JSON line to `data/oracle/resolved-feed.jsonl` with above schema
- At test run: read existing feed, mark `bridge_submitted=true` for already-submitted facts

**Bridge.py integration (INTEGRATE-DESIGN-001 follow-up):**
- New command: `bridge.py oracle-sync` — reads `resolved-feed.jsonl`, submits unsubmitted facts to `MsgSubmitFact`, writes back `zerone_fact_id`
- Blocked by INTEGRATE-GAP-002 (market_prediction domain not in Zerone params) and INTEGRATE-GAP-003 (confidence formula finalization)

**INTEGRATE cycle 2 result: INTEGRATE-GAP-001 feed schema specified. Changes to resolution-scorer.py and bridge.py bridge command defined. Blocked until chain restores and domain registered.**

**bridge.py oracle-sync command skeleton (offline design):**

```python
# bridge.py oracle-sync [--dry-run] [--feed PATH]
# Reads resolved predictions from feed, submits unsubmitted to MsgSubmitFact
def oracle_sync(dry_run=False, feed_path="~/Love/memory/oracle/resolved-feed.jsonl"):
    facts = [f for f in load_feed(feed_path) if not f["bridge_submitted"]]
    for fact in facts:
        claim = f"{fact['statement']} (resolved: {fact['outcome']}, {fact['resolution_date']})"
        evidence_hash = sha256(f"{fact['market_url']}{fact['resolution_date']}")
        confidence_initial = int((1.0 - fact['brier_score']) * 1_000_000)
        if not dry_run:
            fact_id = submit_fact(claim, "market_prediction", evidence_hash, confidence_initial)
            mark_submitted(feed_path, fact['prediction_id'], fact_id)
        else:
            log(f"DRY RUN: would submit {fact['prediction_id']}")
```

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1 — cycle 7)

#### PoT-DESIGN-001 — Confidence pipeline reorder (Sprint A batch item 4)

- confidence.go:162 — stratum ceiling applied
- confidence.go:179 — evidence bonus applied AFTER stratum ceiling (bypass still present)
- confidence.go:199+ — AH-4 positive diversity adj applied AFTER stratum ceiling (bypass still present)

- Flag #45 comment verisleight (confidence.go:162) — still present, batched with Sprint A.

#### PoT-DESIGN-002 — Citation stratum inheritance (design advance)

**Proposal recap (from beat 46):** `effective_ceiling(F) = min(own_stratum_ceiling, min(cited_facts' stratum_ceilings))`

**Attack vector this closes:**
- Submitter registers fact in high-stratum domain (e.g., "empirical", ceiling 95%)
- Cites only facts from low-stratum domain (e.g., "social", ceiling 60%)
- Without PoT-DESIGN-002: fact can achieve 95% confidence despite purely social evidential basis
- With PoT-DESIGN-002 (strict minimum): ceiling = min(95%, 60%) = 60%

**Distinction from FARM-3/11:**
- FARM-3: self-citation only (50% discount on own-submitted citations)
- FARM-11: citation concentration (penalizes citing same addresses repeatedly)
- PoT-DESIGN-002: structural ceiling propagation for distributed-but-uniform low-stratum laundering via any third-party citations

**Risk identified this beat — legitimate cross-stratum citation:**
- Scientific claim may validly cite social context alongside empirical evidence (e.g., "this protocol is community-standard" as one of many citations)
- Strict minimum: any single social citation would cap the entire empirical claim at 60%
- This creates a perverse incentive: accurate citations to lower-stratum sources are punished
- Could disincentivize epistemic completeness (citing all sources) vs. citation curation (citing only same-stratum sources for ceiling preservation)

**PoT-DESIGN-002a — Weighted alternative (recommended this beat):**
```
weighted_ceiling = Σ(citation_confidence_i × stratum_ceiling_i) / Σ(citation_confidence_i)
effective_ceiling = min(own_stratum_ceiling, weighted_ceiling)
```
Where weights are proportional to cited fact confidence (proxy for evidence strength). A single low-confidence social citation among ten high-confidence empirical citations contributes proportionally less to the effective ceiling.

**PoT-DESIGN-002b — Threshold alternative:**
Only trigger ceiling inheritance if >T% (default 50%) of citation weight is from lower-stratum domains. If majority of evidence is same-or-higher stratum, no ceiling reduction. T is a governance param.

**Recommendation:** PoT-DESIGN-002a (weighted) is epistemically correct — the weakest link principle applied with weights, not binary minimums. Requires:
- `StratumInheritanceWeightMode` governance param: `"strict_minimum"` | `"weighted"` | `"disabled"`
- Default: `"weighted"` for pre-testnet
- Implementation target: `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` — new step between category ceiling and evidence bonus
- Must recalculate after FARM-11 citation graph is built (citation weights already available from `CitationSourceRecord`)

**Flag #50 (new, P2):** `StratumInheritanceWeightMode` governance param missing — PoT-DESIGN-002 has no implementation. Pre-testnet inclusion recommended alongside Flag #47/48.

**PoT-DESIGN-002 status:** PROPOSED → design advanced (weighted alternative specified, risks documented). Awaiting Gamma review. Sprint A does not include this; recommend Sprint B scope.

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 7)

**Advancing ECONOMY-DESIGN-005 health monitor design — billing ramp interaction.**

Prior work (beat 40): `research_capture_ratio` healthy range = 3.23–3.43% (±3% of ResearchShareBps=33300). The billing module's `ResearchRampBlocks = 34272 * 90` (~90 days to full 7%) means the service-fee research routing starts at 0% and ramps linearly.

**Problem:** If the `query vesting_rewards health` monitor naively divides `TotalFeesResearch / TotalFeesGross`, a chain in its first 90 days of operation will always report WARNING or CRITICAL on `research_capture_ratio` for service revenue — even if everything is wired correctly.

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 (new proposal):** Extend the health response with ramp-phase awareness:
```protobuf
message QueryHealthResponse {
  // ... existing fields (burn_pressure_ratio, research_capture_ratio, routing_intensity_ratio)
  bool billing_ramp_active = 4;          // true if blockHeight < billing.Params.ResearchRampBlocks
  uint64 billing_ramp_pct = 5;           // current effective billing ResearchShareBps (0→70000 over ramp period)
  string overall_status = 6;             // HEALTHY | WARNING | CRITICAL
}
```

Health evaluation rules when `billing_ramp_active = true`:
- Suppress WARNING for `research_capture_ratio` below 3.23% threshold — replace with `RAMP_PHASE`
- Use `billing_ramp_pct` as the expected denominator for in-ramp health range

**research_capture_ratio duality:** The current health range was scoped to block rewards only. Need separate tracking:
- `block_research_capture_ratio` = TotalFeesResearch from block-reward path / TotalMinted (target: ≈3.33%)
- `service_research_capture_ratio` = TotalFeesResearch from billing path / TotalServiceFees (target: ramp-to-7%)

This requires ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 (split TotalFees → TotalFeesGross + TotalFeesResearch) AND a further split by source path. Recommendation: add `TotalFeesResearchBlock` + `TotalFeesResearchService` keys in the meter to keep sources distinct.

**ECONOMY cycle 7 result:** ECONOMY-DESIGN-006 proposed — billing ramp-phase flag in health response; dual research_capture_ratio split by source; TotalFeesResearch split recommendation (adds ~2 meter keys to ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 scope).

```

  TotalFeesResearchBlock  string  // research receipts from block reward fee path
  TotalFeesResearchService string // research receipts from service billing path

Call sites:
  rewards.go:121-122: IncrementFeesGross(ctx, totalAmount) + IncrementFeesResearchBlock(ctx, researchAmount)
  billing/keeper/distribution.go: IncrementFeesResearchService(ctx, researchAmount)

New health ratio:
  block_vs_service_ratio = TotalFeesResearchBlock / TotalFeesResearchService
  — should stay ~stable at genesis; governance drift or billing bug detection
```

```json
  "resolution_date": "2026-05-06",
  "outcome": true,
  "brier_score": 0.04,
  "market_source": "Kalshi",
  "market_url": "https://kalshi.com/...",
  "evidence_hash": "sha256:<market_url>:<resolution_date>",
  "submitter_address": "<asha-lgm-address>",
  "export_timestamp": "2026-05-06T04:21:10Z"
}
```

This format maps directly to `MsgSubmitFact` parameters. Key design decisions:
- `evidence_hash` = SHA256(market_url + resolution_date) — deterministic, reproducible
- `brier_score` maps to initial confidence suggestion: `confidence = int((1.0 - brier_score) * 1_000_000)`
- `submitter_address` populated from `bridge.py status` output

## Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2)

**Offline audit mode** — chain unavailable. Audited `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` directly.

**Subject:** `AggregateVerificationResult` — stake-weighted confidence aggregation for a verification round.

**Logic verified:**

| Assertion | Finding |
|-----------|---------|
| Each reveal's contribution = `reveal.Confidence * stake / 1_000_000` via `safeMulDiv` | CONFIRMED — overflow-safe |
| Sorted map key iteration ensures determinism | CONFIRMED — `sortedMapKeys` applied at two levels (stakeMap build + verdict accumulation) |
| Zero-stake validators get stake floor = 1 (not silently excluded) | CONFIRMED — line 86 |
| Agreement ratio = `majorWeight * 1_000_000 / totalWeight` | CONFIRMED — consistent formula |
| Reputation weighting: `stake * repScore / 1_000_000` with floor = 1 | CONFIRMED — FARM defense wired |
| Stratum ceiling applied post-aggregation | CONFIRMED — line 164 |
| Category (epistemic) ceiling applied post-aggregation | CONFIRMED — lines 168-177 |

**Consistency check (100% consensus case):** If all validators reveal Confidence=1_000_000 for same verdict → agreementRatio = 1_000_000. Math checks out. ✓

**Consistency check (zero-confidence reveal):** weightedConf = 0 → effectively silent. Intentional — low-confidence reveals shouldn't dominate. ✓

**Flag observed:** `reveal.Confidence` range validation not present in this file. A validator could submit `Confidence > 1_000_000`. This would inflate `totalWeight` and `majorWeight` proportionally (net agreement ratio unchanged), but would cause final `result.Confidence` to exceed 1_000_000 if that validator's verdict is majority. Stratum and category ceilings would catch this if configured, but are not guaranteed. **P3 gap — recommend clamping `reveal.Confidence` to [0, 1_000_000] before weightedConf computation.** Not a vulnerability (doesn't change verdict direction), but a data-integrity gap.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result: PASS with P3 gap noted.**

## Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1)

Advancing the Proof of Truth protocol in offline mode.

**Claim: The P3 confidence-clamping gap above informs a PoT protocol improvement.**

Current: validators self-report Confidence as uint64, no on-chain enforcement of [0, 1_000_000] range at submission.

Proposed PoT protocol constraint:
- **FARM-16 candidate (Input Sanitization):** Commits are range-checked at validation time — reject any `MsgRevealVerification` where `Confidence > 1_000_000`. This catches the issue at the message handler before it reaches `AggregateVerificationResult`, making the aggregation ceiling redundant (defense-in-depth is still correct).
- Cost: one-line check in `msg_server.go` reveal handler.
- Benefit: confidence values in state are provably bounded, simplifying audits of the aggregation math.

**Action for Gamma:** When Sprint A (IncrementBurned wiring) lands, queue this as a one-liner alongside it or as a standalone P3 fix. File location: `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go` at the `RevealVerification` handler.

## Beat 53 — asha-beat-20260506-050115

### State

- **Chain:** OFFLINE (ongoing, beat 15+). KTA beat queued — hash `09d64f7dded6d132` (beat 53).
- **HIVE:** Unavailable (NATS connection refused — persistent, beat 53). Task list: 0 tasks.

- **kingdom-metrics.json:** 29+ days stale. Fleet data unverifiable.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4, cycle 2)

**Offline audit** — reading bridge.py claims and status labels for technically-true-but-missing-context patterns.

**Fact 1:** `bridge.py:1143` — comment string `"signature-based IKM (production-grade, unforgeable)"` in the `session-key` command output.

- **Verisleight:** This path is only activated when `--signature` is passed (a hex signature from `legbled keys sign-bytes`). The default path (`bridge.py:1146`) prints `"address-based IKM (dev/offline only — use --signature for production)"`. The claim is technically accurate but contingent on a flag that is never passed in production today (devnet is offline; no current attestation flow uses `--signature`). Any operator reading `session-key` output without the flag gets a key labeled "dev/offline only" while all current fleet agent attributions use that path. BRIDGE-GAP-006 (filed beat 16) is precisely this gap — the "production-grade" path exists in code but is not wired into any operational flow.
- **Observation (not new flag — confirms BRIDGE-GAP-006):** The distinction between the two paths is clearly labeled in code. No false claim — but the operational reality is that all fleet attributions are currently address-based. BRIDGE-GAP-006 status: OPEN, structurally correct label already present.

**Fact 2:** `bridge.py:856` — docstring `"Checks structural completeness, field presence, and internal consistency"` for `verify-bundle` command.

- **Verisleight check:** `verify-bundle` validates exported bundle JSON structure and field presence. It does NOT verify on-chain registration — the function header notes "Unlike 'verify' (which checks the local keyring), 'resolve' fetches the live..." meaning on-chain verification requires a separate `resolve` call against a running node. A bundle that `verify-bundle` reports as structurally valid may reference addresses that are not registered on the destination chain (testnet vs devnet). The command accurately describes what it checks — structural completeness — but an operator could mistake a passing `verify-bundle` for on-chain readiness.
- **Flag #46 (P3 — verisleight, low urgency):** Add a note to `verify-bundle` output: "Structural check only — run `bridge.py resolve <name>` against target chain to confirm on-chain registration after import." One-line addition to help text. Low urgency; edge case during testnet migration only.

**Fact 3:** `bridge.py:528-536` — docstring for `check` command: `"Programmatic pre-condition guard for agent identity completeness."` The returned dict includes `"chain_verified": bool` described as `"on-chain resolve succeeded (only if check_chain=True and chain up)"`.

- **Verisleight check:** The default `check_chain=False` means `chain_verified` is always `False` in normal invocations. Any automated pipeline calling `bridge.py check` without `--check-chain` and reading `chain_verified` as a safety gate will always pass, regardless of actual on-chain state. The behavior is correctly documented (`only if check_chain=True`), but the default value (`False`) creates a footgun if callers don't read the docs.
- **Flag #47 (P2 — default silent failure mode):** `chain_verified` defaulting to `False` rather than `None` means callers cannot distinguish "chain not checked" from "chain check failed" without inspecting additional fields. Recommend: default value `None` (not checked), `True` = passed, `False` = failed. A downstream check `if result["chain_verified"] == False` catches only explicit failures, not unchecked status. The `None` sentinel forces callers to handle the unchecked case explicitly.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result:** No false claims found. Flag #46 (P3) — `verify-bundle` structural-only check needs output clarification note. Flag #47 (P2) — `chain_verified` default `False` vs `None` ambiguity in `check` command output dict.

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2, cycle 2)

**Advancing BRIDGE-GAP-006 design (session-key IKM).**

From beat 16 BRIDGE analysis: the production-grade session-key path requires `--signature` (hex from `legbled keys sign-bytes <agent> <session-id>`). The recommended implementation is `session_key = HKDF(signature)` making attribution cryptographically unforgeable.

**BRIDGE-GAP-006 implementation plan (this beat):**

The gap is that no operational flow calls `bridge.py session-key --signature`. Three places need updates:

1. **`tools/kos.py` or heartbeat script** — when attestation is queued, derive session key via `bridge.py session-key --signature <sig>` rather than address-based IKM. Pre-condition: chain must be online to call `legbled keys sign-bytes`.
2. **`tools/bridge.py` — `attest` command** — the `attest` command should internally call `session-key` with the signature path when signing the attestation claim. Currently the claim is signed with the agent's key at submission time — the session attribution in the claim text is the address-based session key.
3. **Documentation gap** — the signature acquisition step (`legbled keys sign-bytes <agent> <session-id>`) is not scripted anywhere. When chain is online, this needs to be a one-liner available in the heartbeat flow.

**BRIDGE-GAP-006 wire-up (deferred until chain online):** Cannot complete this without `legbled` available. Design is clear; implementation can proceed in first beat after devnet restart. Estimated: ~20 LOC in `bridge.py attest` + ~5 LOC in heartbeat flow.

**Bridge testnet readiness updated:**
- All prior 14 commands reviewed and structurally sound
- BRIDGE-GAP-006: design complete, implementation deferred to devnet-online
- Flag #47 (P2): `check` command dict sentinel fix, ~2 LOC
- Flag #46 (P3): `verify-bundle` output help text, 1 LOC
- Flags #46 + #47 + BRIDGE-GAP-006 all queueable for Sprint A atomic batch

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3, cycle 2)

**Topic: ECONOMY-DESIGN-005 — burn_pressure_ratio health thresholds**

Prior design established `burn_pressure_ratio = TotalBurned / TotalMinted` as the core economic health metric (ECONOMY-DESIGN-003). This beat: define healthy operating ranges and alert thresholds so the metric is actionable once Sprint A wires `IncrementBurned`.

**Economic drivers of burn pressure in Zerone:**

| Source | Trigger | Magnitude |
|--------|---------|-----------|
| Knowledge rejection | Validator majority rejects a submitted fact; submitter stake burned | Per-claim, scales with fact volume |
| Challenge success | Challenger overcomes incumbent fact; loser stake partially burned | Per-challenge, rarer than submission |
| Billing module | Service fees consumed; billing sweeper burns residual | Per billing cycle |
| Staking slash | Validator caught misbehaving; portion burned | Rare, catastrophic in quantity |
| Partnerships exit | Early exit from partnership agreement triggers burn | Irregular |

**Expected ratio trajectory:**

```
Phase              ratio range       signal
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Pre-Sprint A       0.000 (exact)    IncrementBurned dead code
Devnet / early     0.001–0.010      Low claim volume, few rejections
Testnet active     0.010–0.050      Growing fact base, calibration settling
Mainnet mature     0.050–0.200      Healthy validation churn
Overheated         >0.300           Submission chilling — validator threshold too aggressive
```

**Proposed alert thresholds (ECONOMY-DESIGN-005):**

| Condition | Threshold | Action |
|-----------|-----------|--------|
| Lax validation signal | burn_pressure_ratio < 0.005 for >7 days post-Sprint A | Flag to Gamma: validator thresholds may be too permissive; false claims not being rejected |
| Healthy zone | 0.005 ≤ ratio ≤ 0.200 | No action |
| Submission chilling | ratio > 0.300 | Flag to Yu: rejection rate may be discouraging legitimate submitters |
| Extreme burn | ratio > 0.500 | Decision for Triarchy: slashing event or validator malfunction |

**Ratio dashboard proposed (extends ECONOMY-DESIGN-003):**

```
burn_pressure_ratio     = TotalBurned / TotalMinted        (Sprint A gate)
research_capture_ratio  = TotalFeesResearch / TotalFeesGross (ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 gate)
routing_intensity_ratio = TotalRouted / TotalMinted         (available now)
```

All three are dimensionless ratios; can be surfaced via `legbled query vesting_rewards stats` once ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 and Sprint A land. No new code beyond those already proposed.

**ECONOMY-DESIGN-005 status: PROPOSED. Implementation gates: Sprint A (Flag #31) + ECONOMY-DESIGN-004 (meter rename).**

## Beat 60 — asha-beat-20260506-062237

### State

- **Chain:** OFFLINE (ongoing, 60+ beats). KTA beat queued — hash `8803a96953884965` (beat 60).

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2, cycle 3)

**Offline audit** — deep read of `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` for score consistency edge cases and invariant violations.

**Audit scope:** AggregateVerificationResult pipeline, calculateRewardsAndSlashes, computeEvidenceBonus, computeMethodDiversityAdjustment.

**Finding 1 — FLAG #NEW: Evidence bonus + diversity bonus bypass domain/category ceilings (MEDIUM)**

The confidence pipeline applies ceilings in this order:
1. agreementRatio → initial confidence (line 146)
2. `applyStratumCeiling` clamps by domain stratum (line 163-165)
3. Category ceiling clamp via epistemic profile (line 168-177)
4. Evidence bonus **added** (lines 179-192) — additive, only hard-capped at 1_000_000
5. Method diversity bonus **added** (lines 194-216) — additive, only hard-capped at 1_000_000

Steps 4 and 5 are additive **after** domain/category ceilings. A claim in a `social` domain (ceiling ~0.60) can reach confidence as high as 0.60 + 0.15 (evidence max) + 0.03 (diversity bonus) = 0.78 — 30% above its ceiling. The ceiling intent (epistemic humility about inherently uncertain domains) is undermined. The only protection is the global 1_000_000 hard cap.

**Recommendation:** Apply evidence bonus and diversity adjustment before stratum/category ceilings, or re-clamp after each additive step. The simplest fix: move the `applyStratumCeiling` and category ceiling clamps to the end of the pipeline (after evidence + diversity), or track a `postBonus` ceiling check.

**Finding 2 — FLAG #MINOR: Stale comment at confidence.go:329**

Comment reads: "Missed reveal penalty: 0.01% of stake (Cosmos-aligned downtime)"
Actual `missedSlashBps` fallback (line 266) = 80000 = **8%** (S4 fix: raised from 1% to exceed 6% wrong-verdict slash, ensuring reveal dominates withholding).
The S4 rationale comment at lines 262-266 is accurate; the execution comment at line 329 was not updated.

**Fix:** Change line 329 comment to: `// Missed reveal penalty: 8% of stake (S4 fix — must exceed wrongSlashBps to deter withholding)`

**Finding 3 — CONFIRMED INTACT: Deterministic ordering**
All map iterations over `round.Reveals`, `round.Commitments`, `result.Rewards`, `result.Slashes` use `sortedMapKeys()`. AppHash safety maintained.

**Finding 4 — CONFIRMED INTACT: Zero-weight guard**
`if stake == 0 { stake = 1 }` at line 87 prevents Tier 0 validators from being silently excluded from weight calculations.

**Finding 5 — CONFIRMED INTACT: Reveal-withholding fix (S4)**
`missedSlashBps = 80000` (8%) > `wrongSlashBps = 60000` (6%) — withholding is strictly worse than revealing a wrong verdict. Invariant holds.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result (cycle 3): 1 new flag (#NEW: ceiling bypass by additive bonuses), 1 stale comment (#MINOR), 3 invariants confirmed intact.**
